## 1. Problem

Spec 003 left the site *correct* and *unlookable*. Four locale URLs render with the right `lang`/`dir`, a localised `<title>` and a crawlable `/` chooser, but every document is semantic markup with one `underline` class on it: no colour, no type, no spacing, no header, no footer, no trust layer, and no consent banner. `plan/09` reserves 22–28 Sep 2026 for "`004` design system + layout (tokens, logical CSS, header/footer, trust strip, consent banner, banner)", and three of Phase 0's nine acceptance criteria are unreachable without it: AC 2 (Lighthouse mobile ≥95 with LCP <2.0 s in all four locales), AC 6 (zero fabricated reviews or demo partners on any page — a rule about what the layout is *allowed* to render) and AC 7 (consent banner functional, Consent Mode v2 default-denied, GA4 receiving cookieless pings).

It is also the last cheap moment. Specs 005–011 add a catalogue, corridor pages, category grids, a PDP, a checkout and an admin — every one of them a consumer of tokens, layout primitives, the header, the footer and the trust components. A design system written after them is a refactor of six specs; written now it is the thing they are built against. The same holds for the two gates spec 001 deliberately deferred to this spec and recorded in `docs/architecture.md` §4: the **Content-Security-Policy** (deferred because "the shell loads no script beyond the Next runtime and no third-party origin except Vercel's own preview-feedback script"), and the **Lighthouse job's `continue-on-error: true`** (`ci.yml` line 945: "004 ships the design system — hence `continue-on-error: true` on the job … Spec 004 removes `continue-on-error`"). Both are lifted here or they rot.

Four constraints shape the design more than any of the plan documents do.

**No database, no domain, nothing indexable, no founder provisioning.** Spec 002 is parked (TASK-013 `blocked`), so there is no `country`, no `partner`, no `review`, no `consent_log` and no image in object storage. There is no custom domain and no GA4 measurement id. ADR-0007 keeps everything `noindex` with `Disallow: /` until spec 007. So this spec ships a layout that is honest with **no data at all**: every element that would normally be filled by a row — destination list, florist count, review score, payment logos, company registry number, legal links, product imagery — is either declared as config with a published/unpublished flag and an empty state, or is not rendered. §2 makes that the mechanism, §6 makes "zero links to a non-200 URL" an acceptance criterion, and §8 makes "nothing claimed that we cannot show" a compliance criterion rather than a good intention.

**The client-JS budget is already blown.** `plan/01` §7 caps JS on indexable pages at 120 KB gzipped; the Lighthouse job measures 304 KB of script on `/` today and a performance score of 0.88–0.93 against a 0.95 gate. Roughly 76% of it is the browser Sentry SDK (~146 KB gz) plus zod (~84 KB gz), pulled into every page by `instrumentation-client.ts` → `sentry.client.config.ts` → `@/lib/sentry` → `./logger` → zod. Spec 003's TASK-043 may fix it; if it has not merged when 004 starts, **this spec fixes it in its first task** (§2, §12), because a design system that adds a font, a header, a footer, two client islands and eventually a GA4 tag on top of a 2.5× budget overrun cannot be measured at all. The budget headroom is a designed artefact here, not a hope: §11 carries a bundle table.

**A nonce cannot live in cached HTML.** `plan/01` §9 says "Content Security Policy with nonces for GA4 and payment iframes". A nonce must be unique per response; an ISR/SSG page is one cached response served to everyone, so a nonce baked into it is a public constant and `'strict-dynamic'` built on it is theatre — and generating one per request means rendering every indexable page dynamically, which is a direct hit on priority #1 and #2. This spec therefore splits the policy by render mode (§5.2) and proposes ADR-0016 rather than quietly contradicting the plan (§13 Q5).

**Four open founder decisions, all with defaults.** A9 (brand assets) default is "spec 004 creates a minimal token system" — accepted, and §5.3 says what "minimal" means. A6 (guarantee name and terms) default is "7-day freshness, redeliver-or-refund" — accepted as *copy*, so a rename is a message-catalogue edit and not a code change. A2 (homepage country pick → corridor page) default accepted, and the picker's targets are data. A7 (six guide destinations: DE, FR, ES, IT, RO, NL) default accepted, and the destination list is data. All four are asked in §13; none of them blocks implementation, which is the point of writing them down as data.

## 2. Scope

Each bullet is testable; §9 names the AC.

**Where the design system lives (read this first)**

- A new module, **`src/modules/ui/`**, owned by this spec: tokens documentation, layout primitives, header, footer, trust strip, consent banner, icons, the image wrapper. `plan/01` §5's module list is domain-shaped and has no home for a design system, and `app/` must stay "routes only; thin" while the header and footer are rendered by every route group — so this is a **documented addition** to the module list, not a violation of the boundary rule (§12; §13 Q9 offers `src/components/` as the alternative). It obeys every existing rule: a public `index.ts` barrel, no deep imports, `app/` imports from it and never the reverse, registered in the same PR in `docs/architecture.md` §3 and the `MODULES` manifest of `scripts/check-layout.ts`.
- Tokens are **CSS custom properties declared in a Tailwind v4 `@theme` block** in `src/app/globals.css` (plus `@layer base` resets and `@utility` definitions). There is no `tailwind.config.js` in v4 and none is added. A component never sees a raw colour, size or duration: it uses the generated utilities.
- **Everything data-gated is config, projected for spec 002.** `src/config/countries.ts` (Phase-0 destination list: ISO code, per-locale slug from `plan/02` §4.1, name message key, `corridorPagePublished: false`), `src/config/site-links.ts` (every header/footer link target with the spec that publishes it and a `published` flag), `src/config/company.ts` (legal identity with a `registered: false` state), `src/config/cookies.ts` (the cookie register). Each follows spec 003's precedent exactly: zod-parsed at module load, no database import (`pnpm check:no-db` covers the new files), and a `toCountryRow()`-style projection where spec 002 owns the eventual table, with a unit test pinning the column set so the two cannot drift. `docs/architecture.md` §2 already reserves `countries.ts` for "002/004".

**Tokens (`plan/01` §7, `plan/07` §8, A9 default)**

- **Colour** as *semantic* tokens over a small primitive ramp: `--color-surface`, `--color-surface-raised`, `--color-surface-muted`, `--color-ink`, `--color-ink-muted`, `--color-brand`, `--color-on-brand`, `--color-accent`, `--color-on-accent`, `--color-border`, `--color-border-strong`, `--color-focus`, `--color-success|warning|danger` with their `--color-on-*` partners. Palette intent per `plan/10` §3's locked imagery style guide so the chrome does not fight the photography: a warm neutral-grey ramp (9 steps) as surface and ink, one deep botanical green as brand, one warm blush/terracotta as accent. Exact hex values are the implementer's, subject to the contrast test below, and live in **one** file so a founder swap is one diff.
- **A declared contrast manifest, unit-tested.** `src/modules/ui/tokens/contrast.ts` lists every foreground/background pair a component is allowed to use; a unit test computes the WCAG 2.1 relative-contrast ratio for each and fails below 4.5:1 for body text, 3:1 for ≥24 px or ≥19 px bold text, and 3:1 for UI component boundaries and focus indicators. Adding a component that needs a new pair means adding it to the manifest, which means passing the test. This is `plan/07` §8's "design tokens with contrast-checked palette" as a gate rather than a promise.
- **Type scale**: one variable font, seven steps (`--text-xs` … `--text-4xl`) with `clamp()` fluid sizing, paired line heights and letter-spacing, and a `--measure` (max line length) token. **Spacing**: 4 px base, 9 steps. **Radii**: 4 steps + `--radius-full`. **Shadows**: 3 steps. **Motion**: `--duration-fast|base|slow` and two easings. **Layers**: a named `z-index` scale (`--layer-header`, `--layer-banner`, `--layer-overlay`) so a sticky header, the suggestion banner and the consent sheet cannot fight (they are three of the four elements that overlap in this spec). **Breakpoints and container widths** stay Tailwind's defaults plus a `--container-prose`.
- **No dark mode in Phase 0** (§13 Q3). `color-scheme: light` is declared explicitly so form controls and scrollbars do not render dark on a light page, and because the token names are semantic, adding a `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)` override later is one block in one file and zero component edits. What is rejected is *shipping* it now: it doubles the contrast matrix and every visual baseline for a demo whose job is to sign three Warsaw florists.
- **`fo/no-raw-color`** (new ESLint rule, fixture pairs + RuleTester tests in the spec 001 style): bans arbitrary-value colour utilities (`bg-[#…]`, `text-[rgb(…)]`, `border-[hsl(…)]`, `shadow-[…rgba…]`) and hex/`rgb()`/`hsl()` literals in `className` anywhere in `src/` outside `src/app/globals.css`. Stylelint gains `color-no-hex` and `declaration-property-value-disallowed-list` entries for the same reason, scoped to exclude the `@theme` block. This is the token analogue of `fo/no-physical-css`: one place to change a colour.

**Font (`plan/01` §7: "1 variable font, `font-display: swap`, subset per script")**

- One self-hosted variable font, **Latin + Latin-Ext subsets** (Latin-Ext is non-negotiable: `ą ć ę ł ń ó ś ź ż` for `pl` and the `ro`/`tr` diacritics of Phase 4), weight axis subset to the range actually used, loaded through `next/font/local` from files committed under the repo (no Google Fonts request at runtime — a self-host is both a CWV and a German-court-risk decision, `plan/07` §1.4). Recommended face: **Inter Variable** (SIL OFL, complete Latin-Ext, well-behaved fallback metrics); §13 Q2 asks the founder to confirm or name another.
- `font-display: swap`, `preload` on the subset the locale needs, `adjustFontFallback` (or an explicit `size-adjust`/`ascent-override` fallback stack) so the swap costs no layout shift. Budget: ≤45 KB total font transfer per page, asserted from the build output; no second display face in Phase 0.

**Logical CSS and RTL readiness (`plan/03` §4)**

- Logical utilities only, as spec 001's `fo/no-physical-css` and Stylelint config already enforce; every primitive and component in this spec is written in `ms-/me-/ps-/pe-/start-/end-/text-start` form, and the enforcement is now exercised by real layout rather than by fixtures.
- **`@utility mirror-in-rtl`**: `[dir="rtl"] &{ transform: scaleX(-1) }`, applied to direction-carrying icons (arrow, chevron) and never to the wordmark, a clock, a check or a logo. The icon set ships with the mirroring intent encoded per icon, not decided per call site, so a call site cannot get it wrong.
- The `ar-XB` pseudo-locale visual project (spec 003 TASK-042) becomes a real gate here: for the first time there is a layout for it to mirror, which is exactly the `/review 8` note that "the two visual baselines are byte-identical blanks today".
- The `<bdi>` rule spec 003's runbook records is honoured by the first component that renders user-supplied text — there is none in 004, so the rule is carried into `docs/runbooks/design-system.md` for 009/010.

**Layout primitives and chrome (`plan/04` §3, §15)**

- Primitives: `Container` (max width + inline padding + `--measure` variant), `Stack` (vertical rhythm from the spacing scale), `Row`/`Cluster` (inline with wrap and gap), `Grid` (2-up mobile, 4-up desktop — the card grid 008 will use), `VisuallyHidden`, `SkipLink`. No component ships a bespoke margin: spacing comes from the parent primitive, which is what stops six later specs from inventing six vertical rhythms.
- **Header**: 56 px on mobile, sticky, no CLS (fixed height reserved, not measured after paint); wordmark/logo linking to the locale home (an inline SVG committed to the repo, drawn from A9's default "minimal system" — a typographic wordmark, not an illustrative logo the founder has not approved); primary nav rendered **from `site-links.ts`, only for published targets** (in Phase 0: nothing, so the mobile header is wordmark + locale switcher + currency chip, and the desktop nav appears item by item as 007/008/011 publish pages); the spec 003 `LocaleSwitcher` restyled in place; a **currency chip** that is text only — the locale's default currency ISO code with an `aria-label` from a message key ("Prices shown in {currency}"), server-rendered, identical for every visitor, because the currency *menu* and the `fo_currency` write are spec 008's and must not vary cached HTML (`plan/03` §1, spec 003 §3). Tap targets ≥44 px; no hover-only affordance.
- **Footer**: legal and info links from `site-links.ts` (published only), with the localised paths already fixed in `src/config/locales.ts` reserved as unpublished targets — `/de/impressum`, `/de/rechtliches/agb`, `/pl/regulamin`, `/{loc}/legal/*`; a **company identity block** from `company.ts` that renders the trading name and contact channel now and adds registry code, address and VAT id only when `registered: true` (the OÜ does not exist yet: `plan/09` targets 1 Nov, and inventing a registry number is a `plan/07` §6 offence, not a placeholder); a **`TrustMarks` slot with an explicit empty state** — no payment logos (no live method, and the logos are third-party trademarks), no Trustpilot badge, no review count, no star rating, nothing hidden-until-≥25-reviews because there is nothing to hide (Phase 0 AC 6); and a **"Cookie settings"** control that re-opens the consent banner (`plan/04` §11 requires it to be re-openable from the footer).
- **Trust strip**: server-rendered text, three claims only, each true today — the guarantee (A6 default: "7-day freshness guarantee · redeliver or refund"), "Hand-made by a local florist", "Price includes delivery and VAT" (`plan/04` §3, §12; `plan/07` §4). No Trustpilot line, no delivery-photo claim on a page with no photos, no florist count with no florists. Rendered on the locale home in 004; 008/009 mount the same component on shop pages.
- **404 and 500** documents styled (they exist and are localised from spec 003); the `/` chooser styled; the **skip link** given a visible focused state; a global `:focus-visible` ring from `--color-focus` that meets 3:1 against both adjacent surfaces; `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` disabling every transition and animation the tokens allow.

**Locale-home skeleton (`plan/04` §4, A2 default)**

- Above the fold: `H1` (the **LCP element in Phase 0 is text**, because 006 has not generated any imagery yet — see below), a one-sentence proposition, and the **destination picker**. Below: "How it works" in three steps (You choose → a vetted local florist makes it → photo on delivery), the trust strip, and nothing else. No best-sellers (no catalogue), no occasion module (no calendar data), no delivery-photo gallery (no photos), no reviews carousel (Phase 0 AC 6), no "For florists" teaser until 011 publishes the page.
- The **picker** is server-rendered and usable before hydration (`plan/04` §4): a labelled list of the seven Phase-0 destinations from `countries.ts` in the locale's collation order (`collator(locale)` from spec 003). Each destination whose `corridorPagePublished` is `true` renders as a link to `localePath(locale, "corridorCountry", slug)` — A2's default target, the corridor page; each unpublished destination renders as plain text with a state message ("we are onboarding florists here"). In Phase 0 every flag is `false`, so the picker links nowhere and the site has **zero internal links to a non-200 URL** (AC-14); spec 007 flips flags and the picker becomes navigation with no markup change. Combobox/typeahead enhancement is 007's.
- A **hero media slot** is reserved with a fixed aspect ratio and a token-gradient placeholder, so that when 006 lands imagery the LCP element changes from text to image with **no layout shift and no template edit**. The slot renders no `<img>` in Phase 0 (`plan/10` §3's honesty rule: the demo shows no image it does not have).

**Image conventions (`plan/01` §6, ADR-0014, ADR-0015)**

- `src/modules/ui/media/`: a thin `Media` wrapper over `next/image` fixing the conventions once — `sizes` per named slot (hero 100vw, grid 50vw/33vw/25vw, thumb 96 px), fixed aspect-ratio boxes, `loading="lazy"` + `decoding="async"` below the fold, exactly one `priority` candidate per page (asserted by a lint-free convention check in the gallery test), and `alt` **required** as a prop with no default, because alt text is per-locale data in `product_media` and is never generated at render.
- The **R2 loader seam** of ADR-0015: `src/modules/ui/media/loader.ts` exporting a `next/image` custom loader behind an interface, with a `placeholderLoader` in Phase 0 (no bucket exists) and the R2/variant loader arriving with 002/006 as a swap inside the module — the same seam shape spec 003 used for the locale registry, so no call site changes.

**Consent (`plan/04` §11, `plan/07` §5, Phase 0 AC 7)**

- **Built, not bought** (`plan/07` §5's recommendation, confirmed here; §13 Q6). `src/config/cookies.ts` is the **cookie register**: every cookie we set or intend to set, with name, category (`essential | analytics | marketing`), purpose message key, lifetime, first/third party and the spec that introduces it. Phase 0 rows: `fo_locale` (essential, 365 d, spec 003), `fo_consent` (essential, 6 months on reject / 12 months on accept, this spec), plus the declared-but-not-yet-set `fo_session`, `fo_csrf`, `fo_currency`, `fo_basket` and the GA4/Stripe rows marked "not set in Phase 0". This one file feeds the banner's settings panel, the cookie-policy page 007 will render, the RoPA, and the reviewer's check that no cookie exists outside the register (AC-22).
- **Consent Mode v2, default denied, before anything loads**: a tiny inline `dataLayer`/`gtag` bootstrap setting `ad_storage`, `ad_user_data`, `ad_personalization`, `analytics_storage` (and `functionality_storage`, `personalization_storage`, `security_storage` where honest) to `denied`, `url_passthrough: false`, `wait_for_update` set. It is the only inline script in the app and it is ≤1 KB, which is what makes the CSP conversation in §5.2 concrete.
- **Banner**: bottom sheet on mobile, non-blocking, non-modal, focus not stolen, `Esc`-dismissible, **Accept all / Reject all / Settings as three equal-weight controls** (same size, same contrast, same prominence — no dark patterns, `plan/07` §5), no pre-ticked non-essential box, categories disclosed inline from the register (so the disclosure is complete even before a cookie-policy page exists), reserving no layout space (overlay → CLS delta 0) and re-openable from the footer. Choice persisted in `fo_consent` (first-party, `Path=/`, `SameSite=Lax`, `Secure` outside dev, not `HttpOnly`, JSON-encoded `{v: policyVersion, a: bool, m: bool, ts}` and an unlinkable random `cid`), 6 months for reject / 12 months for accept, written **only on an explicit user action** and never by the server on a cached page (no `Set-Cookie` on any cached response, preserving spec 001 AC-15 and spec 003 AC-12).
- **`ConsentDecision` zod schema + a `ConsentSink` seam**: `POST /api/consent` (`plan/05` #65) validates and hands the decision to a sink. Phase 0 ships `logConsentSink` (one structured log line: `cid`, timestamp, categories, policy version — no IP, no user agent, no page URL, no PII); the durable `consent_log` table of `plan/07` §5 arrives with spec 002 as a second implementation behind the same interface. A forged or unparsable `fo_consent` value is treated as *no consent* and rewritten.
- **GA4 wiring, staged so it needs no founder action to be implemented or tested**: the tag loader is present and reads `NEXT_PUBLIC_GA4_MEASUREMENT_ID`. Unset (Phase 0 CI, local, preview) → no third-party script at all, and a test asserts zero requests to `googletagmanager.com`. Set → the tag loads *after* the Consent Mode default block, sends cookieless pings under default-denied, and calls `gtag('consent','update',…)` when the user accepts. So `plan/09` Phase 0 AC 7 becomes true the moment the founder pastes the id from the week-1 GA4 property into the env store, with **zero code change** — and the event plan, the `consent_state` dimension and the server-side `purchase` event stay spec 023's (§3, §13 Q7).

**Component gallery (`/dev/components`) — decided, not asked**

- A single server-rendered route, `src/app/(dev)/dev/components/page.tsx`, x-default document, `noindex`, rendering every token ramp and every component in every state (default/hover-equivalent/focus/disabled/empty/error). Present only when `ENABLE_DEV_UI` is true; the zod env schema **fails the build** when it is true and `VERCEL_ENV === "production"` (the exact pattern spec 003 established for `ENABLE_PSEUDO_LOCALES`), and the route answers 404 when the flag is off.
- **Not Storybook**: a second build, a second styling entry point, ~100 MB of devDependencies and a second place for a solo founder to keep components alive, in exchange for controls we do not need. The gallery is ~200 lines, uses the app's own build, and doubles as the cheapest possible visual-regression and axe surface: one screenshot and one axe run cover every component, which is why the visual suite gets a real gate here instead of four blank pages.

**Budgets, headers and CI (`plan/01` §7 §9, `plan/12` §5, spec 001 deferred rows)**

- **The inherited client-JS budget is stated and met.** Baseline: 304 KB script on `/`, 0.88–0.93 performance, against 120 KB gz and 0.95. If spec 003's TASK-043 has not already broken zod out of the client Sentry import chain and trimmed the browser SDK, task 2 of this spec does it, and the recorded decision is: **no browser Sentry SDK on public marketing/shop routes in Phase 0** (server-side and edge Sentry unchanged; the client SDK returns on the checkout routes in spec 013, where a client error costs money). §13 Q8 asks the founder to confirm.
- **CSP** (spec 001's deferred row): a static, per-environment policy delivered as response headers from `next.config.ts`, `Report-Only` in this spec with `/api/csp-report` as the collector, plus `Strict-Transport-Security`, `X-Content-Type-Options`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin-when-cross-origin`, `X-Frame-Options`/`frame-ancestors 'none'`, `Permissions-Policy` and `Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy`. `vercel.live` is allowed **on preview only** (the second deferred row, `ci.yml`'s known preview-feedback script) and is absent from the production policy — asserted by a test on both header strings. Shape, and why it is not nonce-based on cached pages, in §5.2.
- **Lighthouse enforced**: `tests/fixtures/seo/lighthouse-urls.json` becomes `["/", "/en", "/en-gb", "/de", "/pl"]` and `continue-on-error: true` is deleted from the `lighthouse` job. Assertions gain `categories:accessibility` and `categories:best-practices` ≥0.95, `total-byte-weight`, `unused-javascript` and an explicit **exclusion of `categories:seo`** with the reason in a comment: the SEO category penalises `noindex`, which is deliberate until spec 007, and nobody may "fix" that score by indexing early.
- **A bundle-budget assertion**: `scripts/check-bundle-budget.ts` reads the build output and fails when first-load JS on any public route exceeds 120 KB gz, when the serialised client message payload exceeds 4 KB gz (spec 003 AC-27, now with real copy), when font transfer exceeds 45 KB, or when a public route's first-load JS grows more than 5 KB against a committed baseline file. It prints the table of §11 to the GitHub step summary.
- **axe on everything new**: `/`, `/en`, `/en-gb`, `/de`, `/pl`, `/dev/components`, a 404, the 500 boundary, and `/ar-XB`; zero serious/critical, no exception list (spec 003 killed the last one and it stays dead). **Visual baselines per platform** (`darwin/` + `linux/`, the convention TASK-035 established) for `/`, the four locale homes at mobile and desktop, the gallery, the consent banner open, the settings panel open, the suggestion banner shown, and `/ar-XB`.

**Messages and docs**

- New namespaces in `messages/en.json`: `nav`, `footer`, `trust`, `consent`, `home`, `company`, `destinations`, plus additions to `a11y` and `common`; `en-gb` overrides only where British wording differs; `de`/`pl` re-drafted by `pnpm i18n:draft` and therefore still unreviewed, so `isLocaleIndexable()` still answers `false` for them (which remains the correct answer — §6). This is the first substantive founder-authored English copy in the repo, and it is what the `plan/13` B12 native reviewers will actually review.
- `messages/en.meta.json`: the four `banner.*` `retained: true` flags are **removed** when TASK-041 ships the banner; `common.floristCount` keeps its flag (the home skeleton renders no florist count because there are no florists — noted for the reviewer, per the TASK-040 carry-forward).
- `docs/runbooks/design-system.md` (new): how to add a token, why a colour must go through the contrast manifest, how to add a component and register it in the gallery, the `<bdi>` and `mirror-in-rtl` rules, how to update a visual baseline, and how to read the bundle table. `docs/architecture.md`: §2 (the new config files and the `(dev)` route group), §3 (the `ui` module row), §4 (**both** the CSP row and the `vercel.live` note removed). `docs/compliance/ropa.md`: the consent-record processing row and a Google Analytics processor row marked inactive-until-configured. README: the new scripts and env vars. `.env.example` + `src/lib/env.schema.ts`: `ENABLE_DEV_UI`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_GA4_MEASUREMENT_ID` (optional), `CSP_REPORT_ONLY`.

## 3. Non-goals

- **Real copy beyond the shell, home skeleton and consent disclosure** → 007 (corridor and info copy), 011 (for-florists). No legal pages are published here: terms, privacy, cookie policy, Impressum, cancellation and guarantee pages are lawyer-gated (`plan/13` B4) and land in 007/hardening. Their URLs are reserved as unpublished `site-links.ts` targets so the footer grows by data.
- **Catalogue, product and shop UI** — cards, grids, filter bars, price blocks, date pickers, gallery, add-ons → 005 (pricing), 008 (category/occasion/shop root), 009 (PDP). 004 ships the `Grid` primitive and the `Media` wrapper they will use, and nothing that knows what a product is.
- **Checkout UI, payment marks, forms and form validation** → 010, 013. No input, select, textarea or form component ships here beyond the consent controls; the design system's form layer is written against the checkout's real fields rather than guessed (§13 Q10 records the risk and the mitigation).
- **The currency menu, `fo_currency` writes, the embedded price table and the client price repaint** → 008. 004 shows the locale's default currency as text.
- **GA4 event schema, the `consent_state` dimension, `web-vitals` RUM and the server-side `purchase` event** → 023. 004 ships only the Consent Mode v2 signals and the gated loader.
- **Dark mode** (§13 Q3), **a second/display typeface**, an illustration or icon system beyond the five icons the chrome needs, and any brand asset the founder has not seen (A9).
- **Storybook**, chromatic, a design-token pipeline (Style Dictionary), and CSS-in-JS. Tokens are CSS variables; the gallery is a route.
- **CSP enforcement** (as opposed to `Report-Only`) and the nonce plumbing for dynamic routes → the flip is a §13 Q5 decision with ADR-0016; the no-store nonce path is written when 010/013 add the first dynamic route that needs it.
- **Corridor pages, sitemaps, canonicals, hreflang tags, JSON-LD and lifting `noindex`** → 007. 004 renders no `alternatesFor()` output and no schema: `Organization` schema needs the OÜ's registry data, which does not exist (`plan/09` week 1).
- **A CMS.** Copy is `messages/*.json` and, from 007, `country_locale_content`.
- **Editing `CLAUDE.md`, `plan/*` or an accepted ADR.** Two tensions are recorded rather than resolved: the `src/modules/ui` addition to `plan/01` §5's module list (§12), and the CSP-nonce deviation from `plan/01` §9 (ADR-0016 proposed, §13 Q5).

## 4. User stories

- As a **buyer on a phone**, the page is legible and branded in under two seconds, the header does not jump as the font loads, and I can pick a destination before any JavaScript has run.
- As a **buyer in the EEA**, the first thing I can do is refuse analytics as easily as accept them, nothing tracks me before I answer, and I can change my mind from the footer on any page.
- As **Googlebot**, the HTML is complete, the layout adds no client-side content, no link I can follow returns a 404, and the page still says `noindex` because nothing here is a true page yet (ADR-0007).
- As the **founder pitching a Warsaw florist in mid-October**, the site looks like a real company and claims nothing it cannot show: no invented reviews, no invented registry number, no invented florist count, no stock photo pretending to be our bouquet.
- As a **screen-reader user**, there is a skip link that becomes visible when focused, one `<h1>`, named landmarks, a focus ring I can see on every surface, and a consent banner that neither traps nor steals my focus.
- As an **implementer agent on spec 008**, there is exactly one grid, one container, one vertical rhythm, one focus ring and one way to render an image, and `pnpm lint` fails the moment I invent a colour.
- As the **reviewer agent**, "does this component meet contrast?" and "is this cookie declared?" are unit tests, and "did the design system blow the budget?" is a table in the CI summary.
- As a **future RTL launch**, `/ar-XB` has been screenshotted against the real header, footer and home layout since Phase 0, so the first Arabic page is a translation job.

## 5. Design

### 5.1 Data model changes (tables, columns, migrations + rollback)

**None. No migration, no rollback file, no database access** — the same deliberate consequence of spec 002 being parked that made spec 003 implementable. `pnpm check:no-db` extends to every file this spec adds (AC-2).

Four config files are shaped as the Phase-0 half of a later table, with a pinned projection and a unit test on the column list, exactly as spec 003 did with `toLocaleRow()`:

| Phase 0 (this spec) | Later owner | Contract that prevents drift |
|---|---|---|
| `src/config/countries.ts` (ISO code, per-locale slug, name key, `corridorPagePublished`) | spec 002 seeds `country`; spec 007 reads `guide_published`/`status` | `toCountryRow()` projects exactly spec 002's `country` columns that exist in Phase 0 (`iso2`, `status`, `guide_published`); a unit test pins the key list |
| `src/config/site-links.ts` (route registry + `published`) | spec 007/008/011 publish entries; spec 012 admin may flip them | one predicate, `isPublished(linkId)`, is the only consumer path; AC-14 forbids rendering an unpublished target as a link |
| `src/config/company.ts` (`registered`, trading name, contact, and the registry/VAT/address fields as optionals) | founder data after OÜ registration (`plan/09` 1 Nov); spec 007's `/legal/company` reads the same object | the type makes the registry fields optional and the component renders them only when present, so a missing number cannot become an empty `<dd>` |
| `src/config/cookies.ts` (the cookie register) | spec 002's `consent_log`; spec 007's cookie-policy page | AC-22: a cookie set anywhere in the app that is not in the register fails a test |

Rollback of the whole spec is `git revert` of its task commits; there is no state to unwind.

### 5.2 API / server actions / jobs / events (zod schemas named)

Two route handlers, no jobs, no events, no server actions.

- **`POST /api/consent`** — `no-store`, `X-Robots-Tag: noindex`, inside spec 001's `/api/` robots disallow. Body parsed by **`ConsentDecisionSchema`** (`{ cid: uuid, policyVersion: int, analytics: boolean, marketing: boolean, decidedAt: ISO datetime }`); rejects anything else with 400 and logs nothing but the failure count. Hands the parsed decision to a **`ConsentSink`** (`record(decision)`); Phase 0 implementation `logConsentSink`. No IP, no user agent, no `Referer`, no page URL is read or stored (§8).
- **`POST /api/csp-report`** — accepts `application/csp-report` and the Reporting-API JSON shape, parsed by **`CspReportSchema`**, and logs **only** `effective-directive` and the *origin* of `blocked-uri` (never the full URL, never `document-uri`'s query, never `script-sample`, which can contain user content). `no-store`, `noindex`, and it never fails a request: an unparsable body is a counter, not a 500.

The `src/modules/ui` public surface:

```
src/modules/ui/
  index.ts            the only import path for everything below
  tokens/             tokens.md (the documented ramp), contrast.ts (the pair manifest), scale.ts (type/space names as types)
  primitives/         Container, Stack, Row, Grid, VisuallyHidden, SkipLink
  layout/             SiteHeader, SiteFooter, PageShell
  trust/              TrustStrip, TrustMarks (empty state), CompanyIdentity
  home/               Hero, HowItWorks, DestinationPicker
  consent/            ConsentBanner (island), ConsentSettings (island), consentState.ts, ConsentDecisionSchema, ConsentSink
  media/              Media, loader.ts (placeholderLoader now, R2 later), slots.ts (the `sizes` table)
  icons/              five inline SVGs with per-icon mirroring intent
```

Other schemas: **`CountryConfigSchema`**, **`SiteLinkSchema`** (id, page type, `published`, owning spec), **`CompanySchema`** (registry fields optional, refined so `registered: true` requires them all — so "registered" cannot be claimed without the data), **`CookieRegistrySchema`** (unique names; every non-essential entry requires a category and a lifetime).

**CSP shape, and why it is not a nonce on cached pages.** The policy is a constant per environment, emitted as a header, and the only inline script in the app is the ≤1 KB Consent Mode default block:

| Directive | Value | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `default-src` | `'self'` | |
| `script-src` | `'self'` + `'sha256-…'` for the consent bootstrap + (`https://www.googletagmanager.com` when GA4 is configured) + (`https://vercel.live` **preview only**) | A build-time **hash** for our one inline script, not a nonce: it is identical in every response, which is precisely why a hash works on a cached page and a nonce does not |
| `style-src` | `'self' 'unsafe-inline'` | React sets `style` attributes and Next inlines critical CSS; documented as a known relaxation with the reason, revisited if either stops being true |
| `img-src` | `'self' data: blob:` + the R2 host when 006 configures it | |
| `font-src` | `'self'` | Self-hosted font; no Google Fonts origin, ever |
| `connect-src` | `'self'` + Sentry ingest + (GA4 when configured) + (`vercel.live` preview only) | |
| `frame-src` | `'none'` in Phase 0; Stripe/3DS origins added by 013 | |
| `frame-ancestors` / `base-uri` / `object-src` / `form-action` | `'none'` / `'self'` / `'none'` / `'self'` | |
| `upgrade-insecure-requests`, `report-uri`/`report-to` → `/api/csp-report` | | Report-Only in this spec |

The trade-off, stated so the reviewer does not have to infer it: an allowlist plus one script hash is **weaker** than `'strict-dynamic'` with a per-request nonce, because any XSS that can inject a `<script src>` to an allowlisted origin (in practice `googletagmanager.com`, which hosts arbitrary container code) is not blocked. What it buys is that every indexable page stays SSG/ISR at the edge, which is priority #1 and #2 and the entire LCP budget. The mitigation is the split: **cached routes get the allowlist policy; `no-store` routes (checkout, account, admin, vendor — all `noindex` and all dynamic by design) get a per-request nonce from `src/proxy.ts` and `'strict-dynamic'`**, written by the spec that ships the first such route. The three highest-value targets for XSS in this product are the checkout and the two authenticated surfaces, and they are exactly the routes that can carry a nonce. §13 Q5 asks the founder to accept this as ADR-0016.

### 5.3 UI (pages, components, states)

| Surface | States | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `SiteHeader` | default · sticky-scrolled · nav-empty (Phase 0) · nav-populated | 56 px mobile, fixed reserved height, no CLS; wordmark → locale home; `LocaleSwitcher` restyled in place (its markup and barrel export unchanged, so spec 003 AC-3 holds); currency chip is server-rendered text; `<header>` landmark, nav named from `a11y` |
| `SiteFooter` | links-empty (Phase 0) · links-populated · company-unregistered · company-registered · trust-marks-empty | Unpublished targets are not rendered at all — never a dead link, never a disabled-looking link; "Cookie settings" re-opens the banner |
| `TrustStrip` | default | Three true claims (A6 default wording); no review, no rating, no count, no photo claim |
| `TrustMarks` | empty (Phase 0) · populated | Empty renders nothing visible and no reserved box, so there is no hole in the footer |
| Locale home | default | H1 (LCP), proposition, picker, how-it-works, trust strip, reserved hero slot with gradient placeholder |
| `DestinationPicker` | published-destination (link) · unpublished-destination (text + onboarding line) · empty (no destinations configured) | Works with JS disabled; locale-collated; A2's default target |
| `ConsentBanner` (island) | hidden (decision recorded) · shown · settings-open · saved | Bottom sheet mobile / inline card desktop; three equal controls; non-modal; `Esc` closes without deciding (and it returns on the next page view, because dismissal is not consent); `aria-live="polite"`; CLS delta 0 |
| `ConsentSettings` (island) | default (all non-essential off) · dirty · saved | Per-category toggles, essential locked-on with the reason; every cookie in the category listed from the register with purpose and lifetime |
| Suggestion banner (spec 003 TASK-041) | as spec 003 defines | Restyled only: tokens, spacing, z-layer under the consent sheet. No behaviour change |
| `/` chooser | default | Styled; still zero client JS, still `noindex,follow` (spec 003 AC-7 preserved) |
| 404 / 500 | localised (spec 003) | Styled; status codes preserved; each offers the locale home and, once published, the destination hub |
| `/dev/components` | flag-on gallery · flag-off 404 | Every token ramp and component state; the visual + axe surface |

Accessibility rules that are design decisions, not afterthoughts: one `<h1>` per document; landmarks (`banner`, `main`, `contentinfo`, named `navigation`s); focus ring visible on every token surface (contrast-tested); tap targets ≥44 px; text resizable to 200% without loss (no fixed-height text containers); no colour-only meaning; the sticky header must not obscure a focused element (scroll-margin on focusable targets); `prefers-reduced-motion` honoured; the consent sheet does not permanently obscure content (WCAG 1.4.13, 2.2.2).

### 5.4 Rendering & caching (per plan/01 §3)

- `/` chooser: **SSG**, unchanged, still zero application JS.
- `/{locale}` home: **ISR**, `revalidate` 3600, tag `home:{locale}` — the wiring spec 003 already put in place; 004 adds content to it and no new data source, so nothing is invalidated at runtime and `src/lib/cache.ts` stays the noop seam.
- 404 / 500: static per locale. `/dev/components`: static, flag-gated, `noindex`.
- `/api/consent`, `/api/csp-report`: dynamic, `no-store`.
- **No response varies by request header and no cached response sets a cookie.** The header, footer, trust strip and picker are server components inside the cached HTML; the two client islands (consent, language suggestion) decide from `document.cookie` and `navigator.languages` after hydration. So there is still one cache entry per path, no `Vary`, and no `Set-Cookie` on a cached page (`plan/02` §14, spec 001 AC-15, spec 003 AC-12).
- Client JS added: the consent island (+ settings, dynamically imported on open), the suggestion island, and the ≤1 KB inline consent bootstrap. Against that, the browser Sentry SDK and zod leave the public routes. Net effect is a **reduction**, and §11's table is the evidence.

## 6. SEO considerations (mandatory)

- **Indexability is unchanged: nothing becomes indexable here.** `src/app/robots.ts` keeps `Disallow: /`, the non-production blanket `X-Robots-Tag: noindex` stays, localised documents keep `noindex,nofollow` and `/` keeps `noindex,follow` until spec 007 lifts them by rule (ADR-0007). `/dev/components` is `noindex` and env-absent in production. A styled site is not a true page.
- **Canonical, hreflang, sitemaps, schema: none emitted** (spec 007). The preconditions are preserved rather than advanced: one URL form per (locale, path), `localePath()` as the only URL builder, `alternatesFor()` untouched and still filtered by `isLocaleIndexable()`.
- **Internal links are the real SEO content of this spec.** `plan/02` §11 wants crawl depth ≤3 and authority flowing from the locale home to corridor pages; `plan/09` Phase 0 AC 1 wants production to index *only* true pages. 004 builds the graph's frame and leaves it empty on purpose: header nav, footer link lists and the destination picker all render from `site-links.ts` / `countries.ts` and render **nothing** for an unpublished target. Consequences that are the point: **zero internal links to a non-200 URL** (AC-14, checked by an e2e crawl of every link in the header, footer and home of all four locales), zero soft-404s, no phantom URL space for a crawler to discover, and — when 007 flips `guide_published` — a picker and a footer that become navigation with no template edit, which is `plan/09`'s "a new country is data" promise applied to the layout.
- **Thin-content risk.** Four locale homes whose `de`/`pl` copy is an echoed English draft is duplicate content, and this spec is the first one that writes enough copy for that to matter. Three guards, all inherited and all still true: nothing is indexable (robots disallow); `isLocaleIndexable()` returns `false` while a locale's unreviewed share exceeds 5%, which is why the newly drafted `nav`/`footer`/`trust`/`consent`/`home` keys **keep** `de` and `pl` out of hreflang and sitemaps until a native reviewer works the queue; and the home skeleton deliberately renders no module it has no data for, so there is no boilerplate block repeated across locales to be thin *with*.
- **CWV budget impact — this spec's largest SEO effect.** It is the first to add a font, a sticky header and two client islands, and the first where the budgets are *enforced*: `continue-on-error` comes off the Lighthouse job, the URL set grows to `/` plus all four locale homes, and LCP <2.0 s / CLS <0.05 / script ≤120 KB gz / performance ≥0.95 become merge gates (`plan/01` §7, `plan/12` §5, Phase 0 AC 2). Specifically: the LCP element is server-rendered text with the font preloaded and fallback metrics matched, so there is no image dependency and no swap shift; the header's height is reserved, not measured; both banners overlay rather than reflow (CLS delta 0); the picker needs no hydration to work; no third-party script loads before consent and none at all while GA4 is unconfigured. `categories:seo` is deliberately **not** asserted, because the SEO audit penalises `noindex` and that penalty is correct until 007 — recorded in `lighthouserc.json` so nobody removes `noindex` to make a number go green.
- **Crawl efficiency.** No redirects exist to chain; unknown locales still 404 (spec 003 AC-8); the gallery and the pseudo-locales are absent from production by env validation.

## 7. i18n considerations (mandatory)

- **New message keys / namespaces.** `nav.*` (menu label, each published nav item, mobile menu open/close), `footer.*` (section headings, legal link labels, cookie-settings control, company block labels), `trust.*` (the three claims, with the guarantee name as its own key so A6's rename is a catalogue edit), `consent.*` (headline, body, accept/reject/settings labels, per-category names and purposes, save, the "essential cookies cannot be disabled" reason, the re-open control), `home.*` (H1, proposition, picker label and help text, the onboarding state line, how-it-works steps), `company.*` (identity labels), `destinations.*` (the seven country names — UI strings until spec 002 seeds CLDR/`country_translation`), plus `a11y.*` (skip link target name, header/footer landmark names, "current page") and `common.*` additions. All ICU, namespaced, typed; `fo/no-literal-strings` stays clean with zero `eslint-disable`.
- **Locale formatting.** Everything goes through spec 003's `format.ts`; 004 adds no `Intl` call and no formatter (`fo/no-adhoc-intl` guarantees it). The currency chip prints the ISO code from `locale.currencyDefault` with a message-key `aria-label`; the destination list is ordered by `collator(locale)`, so Polish `ł` sorts correctly in `pl` — the one place formatting is visible in this spec, and it is tested.
- **RTL impact.** This is the spec that makes `plan/03` §4 real: logical properties throughout (already lint-enforced), `mirror-in-rtl` for the two direction-carrying icons with per-icon intent, `dir` inherited from spec 003's document, and the `ar-XB` visual project screenshotting the header, footer, home, consent banner and gallery. No Arabic font is loaded (`plan/03` §4).
- **Address/phone formats.** Not touched (no form ships); `plan/03` §8's data and formatter stay spec 003's and 010's.
- **Translation review plan and `noindex` gating.** 004's English copy is authored by the founder and is the source of truth; `pnpm i18n:draft --locale de|pl` re-drafts, so `de`/`pl` stay `source: "machine"`, `reviewed: false`, their unreviewed share stays >5%, and `isLocaleIndexable()` keeps answering `false` — the honest answer for a German page showing English text (`plan/03` §6.4, `plan/02` §12). Nothing in this spec is indexable anyway, so the gate costs nothing now and is the mechanism 007 depends on. Two notes for the `plan/13` B12 reviewers, recorded in the glossary: the guarantee name (A6) is a brand term whose translation must be decided once, not per string; and `pl` uses the informal `Ty` register in UI copy per A5's resolved default.
- **Length and truncation.** German compounds and the +40% `en-XA` pseudo-locale are the reason the header, the picker and the consent controls are specified to wrap rather than truncate, and the reason `en-XA` is in the visual matrix.

## 8. Compliance considerations (mandatory)

- **Data flows added → RoPA.** Two. (1) **Consent records**: an unlinkable random `cid`, a timestamp, the category choices and the policy version, written to a structured log line in Phase 0 and to `consent_log` from spec 002. Lawful basis: **legal obligation** (GDPR Art. 7(1) — we must be able to prove consent); retention 5 years after withdrawal per `plan/07` §1.2; no IP, no user agent, no page URL, so the record is not linkable to a person by us. (2) **Google Analytics 4** as a processor, added to the RoPA and the privacy-policy processor list as **declared but inactive** while `NEXT_PUBLIC_GA4_MEASUREMENT_ID` is unset, and active the moment it is set — the founder flipping that env var is a RoPA-affecting act and is documented as such in the runbook. `docs/compliance/ropa.md` is updated in the same PR; the reviewer confirms at PASS.
- **Lawful basis and consent gating (ePrivacy Art. 5(3) as transposed: EE ECA, DE TDDDG, PL Telecom, UK PECR).** No non-essential storage or access happens before consent: no third-party script loads, no analytics cookie is set, and the only pre-consent storage is the essential `fo_locale` (spec 003) and `fo_consent` itself, both first-party, both set only on an explicit user action, both in the register. Consent Mode v2 defaults to denied for `ad_storage`, `ad_user_data`, `ad_personalization` and `analytics_storage` with `url_passthrough` off, so GA4 — when configured — can only send cookieless pings until an accept (`plan/04` §11, `plan/07` §5, Phase 0 AC 7). Withdrawal is one control in the footer on every page. Consent is not inferred from dismissal: pressing `Esc` records nothing and the banner returns.
- **No dark patterns.** Accept / Reject / Settings are equal in size, weight and contrast (asserted by a visual baseline and by a DOM assertion on the computed styles, AC-20); no pre-ticked non-essential category; no "legitimate interest" pre-consent analytics; no cookie wall; the banner never blocks content or focus. Lifetimes are asymmetric in the user's favour only in the direction the guidelines expect: 6 months for reject, 12 for accept.
- **Price display (`plan/07` §4).** No price is rendered in this spec. What it does ship is the *claim* — "price includes delivery and VAT" — in the trust strip, and the rule that makes it keepable: the claim is a message key placed by this spec on pages that show no price, and 008/009 render prices only through `formatMoney` on integer minor units, so the strip cannot end up next to a price that excludes VAT. No drip pricing exists to disclose yet; no "was/now" component ships (Omnibus 30-day rule) precisely so that 005 must build it deliberately.
- **Consumer information (`plan/07` §6).** The footer is the site-wide identity surface, and it tells the truth about a company that does not exist yet: trading name and contact channel now; registry code, address and VAT id only when `company.registered` is true, enforced by the schema refinement so the flag cannot be set without the data. `/de/impressum`, `/de/rechtliches/agb` and `/pl/regulamin` are reserved as unpublished targets on the paths the German and Polish regimes expect, so the Impressum lands on the right URL in 007 rather than being retrofitted.
- **Reviews and trust (`plan/07` §7, Phase 0 AC 6).** Zero reviews, zero testimonials, zero ratings, zero Trustpilot widget or score, zero star icons, zero "1,240 customers" copy, zero demo partner name or photo, zero delivery-photo claim. `TrustMarks` renders an empty state and the visual baseline records that it is empty. AC-15 makes this a grep-able, testable fact rather than a design intention, which is what `plan/09` AC 6 asks for.
- **Accessibility (EAA / WCAG 2.1 AA, `plan/07` §8).** The conformance work of this spec is the design system: a contrast-tested palette (1.4.3, 1.4.11), a visible focus indicator on every surface (2.4.7), keyboard operability of the only interactive components that exist (2.1.1), a visible skip link (2.4.1), reflow and 200% text without loss (1.4.4, 1.4.10), reduced-motion respect (2.3.3), 44 px targets, named landmarks and one `<h1>` (1.3.1, 2.4.6), non-modal non-obscuring banners (1.4.13, 2.2.2), and no colour-only meaning (1.4.1). axe runs on all four locale homes, `/`, the gallery, a 404, the 500 boundary and `/ar-XB` with **no exception list**. A manual VoiceOver pass over the home and the consent flow is a phase-gate item recorded in the runbook, because axe cannot judge the equal-weight requirement or a focus order.
- **Logs and PII.** No PII enters a log, a URL, an analytics call or a cookie. The consent record is an unlinkable id; `/api/csp-report` logs a directive name and a blocked *origin* and explicitly discards `document-uri`, `script-sample` and any query string (a CSP report is a classic accidental PII leak); the consent island transmits no page URL; `fo_consent` contains no identifier beyond its own random id. GA4, when configured, receives the `plan/04` §14 dimensions and no personal field — enforced by spec 023, declared here.
- **Security.** CSP `Report-Only` plus HSTS, `X-Content-Type-Options`, `Referrer-Policy`, `frame-ancestors 'none'`, `Permissions-Policy` and COOP land here (spec 001's deferred row). `vercel.live` is allowed on preview only and absent from production, asserted on both header strings. `ENABLE_DEV_UI` is refused in production by the env schema, so the gallery cannot be exposed by configuration mistake. No new dependency handles a secret; the font is committed, not fetched.

## 9. Acceptance criteria

Tokens, font, logical CSS
- **AC-1** `src/app/globals.css` declares the full token set of §2 in one `@theme` block; a unit test asserts every documented token name resolves to a non-empty value in the built CSS, and that no component file contains a hex, `rgb()`, `hsl()` or arbitrary-value colour utility (`pnpm lint` fails a `bg-[#ff0000]` fixture and passes the equivalent token utility).
- **AC-2** `pnpm build` and `pnpm test` succeed with `DATABASE_URL` unset and with no network access; `pnpm check:no-db` covers every file this spec adds; no file added imports `src/lib/db*`, `drizzle*`, `pg` or `postgres`.
- **AC-3** Every pair in `tokens/contrast.ts` meets its threshold (4.5:1 body, 3:1 large text, 3:1 UI boundary and focus indicator), computed by a unit test from the token values themselves; changing a token to a failing value fails the test with the pair named. A component using a foreground/background combination absent from the manifest fails the same test.
- **AC-4** Exactly one variable font family is loaded, self-hosted, with Latin and Latin-Ext coverage (`ą ć ę ł ń ó ś ź ż` render from the webfont, not a fallback, asserted in the visual `pl` baseline), `font-display: swap`, preloaded, with fallback metrics set; total font transfer per page ≤45 KB (build-output assertion); no request to `fonts.googleapis.com` or `fonts.gstatic.com` from any page (e2e network assertion).
- **AC-5** `pnpm lint` reports zero `fo/no-physical-css` and zero Stylelint direction violations across all new CSS and components; `@utility mirror-in-rtl` flips the two direction-carrying icons under `dir="rtl"` and leaves the wordmark and the check icon unflipped (visual assertion on `/ar-XB`).
- **AC-6** `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` removes every transition and animation the design system defines (asserted by an e2e run with the media feature emulated: no animated property changes on banner open).

Chrome, layout, home
- **AC-7** `SiteHeader` renders on every localised document at a reserved 56 px mobile height with no cumulative layout shift attributable to it (CLS ≤0.05 overall, and the header's box is identical before and after hydration); the wordmark links to the locale home; the locale switcher's markup and the `src/modules/i18n` barrel exports are unchanged (spec 003 AC-3 still passes).
- **AC-8** The currency chip renders the locale default (`en`→EUR, `en-gb`→GBP, `de`→EUR, `pl`→PLN) as text with a localised `aria-label`, is identical for every visitor of that URL (byte-identical body hash with and without an `fo_currency` cookie), and writes no cookie.
- **AC-9** `SiteFooter` renders the "Cookie settings" control on every localised document; it re-opens the consent banner after a decision has been recorded; the company block renders the trading name and contact channel and **no** registry code, VAT id or address while `company.registered` is false; a fixture with `registered: true` and a missing registry code fails `CompanySchema` at module load.
- **AC-10** The locale home renders H1 + proposition + destination picker + how-it-works + trust strip, in all four locales, with one `<h1>`, named `banner`/`main`/`contentinfo` landmarks, and the reserved hero slot present but containing no `<img>`.
- **AC-11** The destination picker lists the seven Phase-0 destinations (PL + A7's six) in the locale's collation order (`pl` orders `ł` correctly), is fully usable with JavaScript disabled, renders every destination as plain text with the onboarding line while `corridorPagePublished` is false, and renders a link to `localePath(locale,"corridorCountry",slug)` when a fixture flips one flag to true — with **no change under `src/app/`** (the "a country is data" proof).
- **AC-12** The `/` chooser, the localised 404 and the 500 boundary are styled with the design system and keep their spec-003 contracts exactly: `/` is 200, `noindex,follow`, zero application JS, zero `Set-Cookie`, no `Vary`; 404 is status 404; the 500 boundary renders without the client provider.
- **AC-13** The spec 003 suggestion banner is restyled with tokens and renders **below** the consent sheet in the z-layer scale; its behaviour matrix (spec 003 AC-28) still passes unchanged.

SEO and honesty gates
- **AC-14** An e2e crawl of every `<a href>` in the header, footer and home of all four locales plus `/` finds **zero** links to a non-200 URL and zero links to an unpublished `site-links.ts` target; `robots.txt` still `Disallow: /`; every localised document still `noindex,nofollow` and `/` still `noindex,follow`.
- **AC-15** No rendered page contains a review, rating, star, testimonial, review count, Trustpilot mark, partner name, partner photo, delivery-photo claim or florist count (Phase 0 AC 6), asserted by a DOM/text assertion over all four locale homes and the footer, and by a repo grep over `messages/en.json` for the forbidden claim shapes.
- **AC-16** No JSON-LD, canonical or hreflang tag is emitted by any page in this spec (spec 007 owns them); `alternatesFor()` output and `isLocaleIndexable()` answers are unchanged by this spec's message additions except that `de`/`pl` remain non-indexable with an unreviewed share >5%.

Consent
- **AC-17** With no `fo_consent` cookie, the banner appears after hydration on a localised page, reserves no layout space (CLS delta 0), does not steal focus, is reachable and operable by keyboard, closes on `Esc` **without recording a decision** (and returns on the next page view), and blocks no content.
- **AC-18** Before any decision: `dataLayer` contains a Consent Mode **default** entry with `ad_storage`, `ad_user_data`, `ad_personalization` and `analytics_storage` all `denied` and `url_passthrough: false`; **zero** network requests are made to any third-party origin (asserted by an e2e request interceptor); no cookie other than `fo_locale`/`fo_consent` exists.
- **AC-19** "Accept all" sets `fo_consent` with `Max-Age` ≈12 months and issues a `gtag('consent','update',…)` granting analytics; "Reject all" sets it with ≈6 months and issues no grant; "Settings" → analytics on, marketing off saves exactly that; in all three cases `POST /api/consent` receives a body that parses under `ConsentDecisionSchema`, and a forged or unparsable `fo_consent` is treated as no consent and rewritten.
- **AC-20** Accept, Reject and Settings have equal computed prominence — same rendered width class, same font size and weight, and both text/background pairs meeting 4.5:1 — asserted from computed styles, not from a screenshot alone; no non-essential category is pre-enabled; the settings panel lists every register entry in the category with its purpose and lifetime.
- **AC-21** With `NEXT_PUBLIC_GA4_MEASUREMENT_ID` unset, no request is made to `googletagmanager.com` and no GA4 script tag exists in the document; with it set in a test environment, the tag is requested **after** the default-denied block and the page still meets the script budget of AC-25.
- **AC-22** Every cookie the application sets appears in `src/config/cookies.ts` with a category and a lifetime; a test that collects cookies from an e2e session (including after accept) and compares against the register fails when a cookie is set that is not registered, or when a registered essential cookie is missing its purpose key.

Budgets, headers, CI, docs
- **AC-23** A `Content-Security-Policy-Report-Only` header is sent on every HTML response with the §5.2 directive set; `vercel.live` appears in the **preview** policy and is absent from the **production** policy (both header strings asserted); HSTS, `X-Content-Type-Options`, `Referrer-Policy`, `frame-ancestors 'none'`, `Permissions-Policy` and COOP are present; `POST /api/csp-report` accepts both report shapes, logs only the directive and the blocked origin, never logs `document-uri`, `script-sample` or a query string, and never returns 5xx for a malformed body.
- **AC-24** The `lighthouse` CI job has no `continue-on-error`, runs against `["/", "/en", "/en-gb", "/de", "/pl"]`, and passes: performance ≥0.95, accessibility ≥0.95, best-practices ≥0.95, LCP <2000 ms, CLS <0.05, script transfer ≤122 880 B, image transfer ≤204 800 B; `categories:seo` is explicitly not asserted, with the `noindex` reason recorded in `lighthouserc.json`.
- **AC-25** `scripts/check-bundle-budget.ts` passes: first-load JS ≤120 KB gz on every public route, serialised client message payload ≤4 KB gz, font transfer ≤45 KB, and no public route regressed more than 5 KB gz against the committed baseline; `/` still ships zero application JS; the browser Sentry SDK and zod are absent from every public route's client bundle (asserted from the build output, not by inspection).
- **AC-26** axe reports zero serious/critical violations, with **no** exception list, on `/`, `/en`, `/en-gb`, `/de`, `/pl`, `/dev/components`, a 404 and `/ar-XB`; the 500 boundary is included.
- **AC-27** Visual baselines exist for both platforms (`darwin/`, `linux/`) at 0.1% for: `/`, each locale home at mobile and desktop, the gallery, the consent banner shown, the settings panel open, the suggestion banner shown, and `/ar-XB`; the `pseudo-rtl` project screenshots real layout (not a blank page).
- **AC-28** `/dev/components` renders every token ramp and every component state when `ENABLE_DEV_UI` is true, returns 404 when it is false, and `pnpm build` **fails** when it is true while `VERCEL_ENV=production`; `pnpm env:check` is green and `.env.example` agrees with the zod schema for all three new variables.
- **AC-29** `pnpm i18n:check` is green: the new namespaces exist in `en`, `en-gb` carries only genuine overrides, `de`/`pl` are re-drafted deterministically, no key is unused (the four `banner.*` `retained` flags are removed; `common.floristCount` keeps its flag with the reason recorded), and the per-locale reviewed-share summary shows `de`/`pl` non-indexable.
- **AC-30** Docs are current: `docs/architecture.md` §2 lists the new config files and the `(dev)` route group, §3 has the `ui` module row, and §4 contains **neither** the CSP row nor the `vercel.live` note; `docs/runbooks/design-system.md` exists and is indexed; `docs/compliance/ropa.md` has the consent-record row and the inactive-GA4 processor row; README documents the new scripts and env vars; `tests/unit/architecture-doc.test.ts` and `tests/unit/docs.test.ts` are green.

## 11. Observability

- **CI is the observability surface for the performance contract**, and it becomes a blocking one here. The `lighthouse` job loses `continue-on-error` and writes a step summary of performance / accessibility / best-practices score, LCP, CLS and script transfer **per URL** for the five URLs, so a regression names the locale it happened in.
- **The bundle table** is printed by `scripts/check-bundle-budget.ts` to the step summary on every PR and committed as a baseline file so the diff is reviewable:

| Route | First-load JS (gz) | Client messages (gz) | Fonts | Budget |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `/` | 0 KB app JS | 0 | ≤45 KB | zero app JS |
| `/{locale}` (home) | ≤120 KB | ≤4 KB | ≤45 KB | `plan/01` §7 |
| `/dev/components` | not budgeted (non-production) | — | — | — |

- **CSP violations** arrive at `/api/csp-report` as structured log lines (`effective-directive`, blocked origin, environment) with no PII, which is how the Report-Only period produces the evidence for the enforce flip of §13 Q5. A spike is visible in the log drain; no alert is configured (Phase 0 has no on-call; `plan/09` spec 025 owns alerting).
- **Consent** produces one log line per decision (`cid`, categories, policy version) — the Phase-0 stand-in for `consent_log` and the only counter the founder needs before analytics exist. It is deliberately not an analytics event: `plan/04` §14's `consent_update` event is spec 023's, and its name is recorded there so it is not invented twice.
- **Logs.** No new request-scoped field; `request_id` and `locale` continue to carry every line. No header value, cookie value or URL query is ever logged.
- **Sentry.** Server and edge unchanged. The browser SDK is removed from public routes (§2, §13 Q8), which is a deliberate reduction in client-error visibility on marketing pages, accepted because CWV is priority #1 and server-side errors — the ones that break a page — are still captured. It returns on the checkout routes in spec 013.
- **Alerts.** None. The nearest thing is a red required check: a token that fails contrast, a cookie outside the register, a link to a 404 or a blown budget is a failed build rather than a production incident, which is the point of putting all four in CI.

## 12. Rollout

- **Feature flags.** Three, all data or env, none behavioural at runtime: `site-links.ts` `published` (per link target), `countries.ts` `corridorPagePublished` (per destination), and `ENABLE_DEV_UI` (env, refused in production). `NEXT_PUBLIC_GA4_MEASUREMENT_ID` behaves as a flag by presence. As in spec 003 §12, the config-file flags are a **bounded, stated deviation** from `CLAUDE.md`'s "go-live is a data flip in admin, never a code change": there is no database and no admin yet, the flags are read only through one predicate each, and spec 002's `country`/`feature_flag` plus spec 012's admin take over the authority with no call-site change (AC-11 is the proof). No ADR is proposed for that; ADR-0016 is proposed only for the CSP shape (§13 Q5).
- **Environments.** local (`ENABLE_DEV_UI=true`, `CSP_REPORT_ONLY=true`, GA4 unset) → PR preview (`ENABLE_DEV_UI=true`, `ENABLE_PSEUDO_LOCALES=true`, GA4 unset, `vercel.live` allowed, protected, `noindex`) → production alias (`ENABLE_DEV_UI=false` enforced by the env schema, `vercel.live` absent from the policy, GA4 as configured, still `Disallow: /` until spec 007). No custom domain; no founder provisioning is required for any task in this spec to be implemented, tested and reviewed.
- **Migration order.** None (no database). One ordering constraint against spec 003: if TASK-043 has not merged, task 2 below carries its AC-27 work, and TASK-043 must then not re-litigate the Sentry/zod decision.
- **Suggested task plan for `/plan-tasks` — six tasks, two independent pairs.** Each is one PR, ≤1 day, zero founder actions.

```
T1 tokens + font + logical CSS + gallery shell   ─┬─────────────► T3 primitives + header + footer + registries ─┬─► T5 home skeleton + trust strip + styling pass ─┬─► T6 gates + docs + close
                                                  │                                                             │                                                  │
T2 client-JS budget + CSP + security headers ─────┘                T4 consent banner + register + GA4 seam ──────┘                                                  │
                                                  └──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

  1. **Tokens, font, logical CSS, gallery shell.** `@theme` token set, contrast manifest + test, `fo/no-raw-color` + Stylelint additions, the self-hosted variable font with subsets and fallback metrics, `@layer base` reset, focus ring, reduced-motion, `@utility mirror-in-rtl`, the icon set, `/dev/components` route + `ENABLE_DEV_UI`. Covers AC-1, AC-3, AC-4, AC-5, AC-6, AC-28 (partial). **No dependencies — starts immediately.**
  2. **Client-JS budget, CSP and security headers.** The Sentry/zod client-chain fix (browser SDK off the public routes), the per-environment CSP `Report-Only` header set in `next.config.ts`, the other five security headers, `POST /api/csp-report`, `CSP_REPORT_ONLY` env. Covers AC-23 and the budget precondition of AC-25. **Independent of task 1 — runs in parallel** (it touches config, CI and `src/lib`, not CSS or components).
  3. **Primitives, header, footer, registries.** `Container`/`Stack`/`Row`/`Grid`/`VisuallyHidden`/`SkipLink`, `SiteHeader` (wordmark, nav from registry, restyled switcher, currency chip), `SiteFooter` (link registry, company identity, `TrustMarks` empty state, cookie-settings control), `src/config/countries.ts` / `site-links.ts` / `company.ts` with schemas and projections, `nav`/`footer`/`company`/`destinations` messages. Covers AC-7, AC-8, AC-9, AC-14 (structure). **Depends on 1.**
  4. **Consent banner, cookie register, Consent Mode v2, GA4 seam.** `src/config/cookies.ts`, `ConsentDecisionSchema` + `ConsentSink` + `logConsentSink`, `POST /api/consent`, the inline default-denied bootstrap, the banner and settings islands, `fo_consent`, the GA4 loader gated on env and consent, `consent` messages, the RoPA rows. Covers AC-17 to AC-22. **Depends on 1; independent of 3 — runs in parallel with it.**
  5. **Home skeleton, trust strip, styling pass, media conventions.** Hero (text LCP + reserved slot), how-it-works, `DestinationPicker`, `TrustStrip`, styled `/` chooser / 404 / 500, restyled suggestion banner, `Media` + `slots.ts` + `placeholderLoader`, `home`/`trust` messages. Covers AC-10, AC-11, AC-12, AC-13, AC-15, AC-16. **Depends on 3** (and on 1); the suggestion-banner restyle additionally needs spec 003's **TASK-041** merged — if it has not, that half is dropped from this task and picked up by task 6 rather than blocking the home page.
  6. **Gates, budgets, docs, close.** Lighthouse URL set + assertions + `continue-on-error` removed, `scripts/check-bundle-budget.ts` + baseline + step summary, axe URL set extended, visual baselines committed for both platforms, `i18n:check` green with the `retained` flags cleaned, `docs/architecture.md` §2/§3/§4, `docs/runbooks/design-system.md`, README, `.env.example`, spec §14. Covers AC-24, AC-25, AC-26, AC-27, AC-29, AC-30. **Depends on 2, 4 and 5** — it is the task that measures the finished thing, which is why the enforcement flip is last.
- **Rollback plan.** Every task is one squash commit over a stateless change set; `git revert` restores the previous state exactly (no migration, no data, no external resource). Revert order is the inverse of the task order. Two specific hazards: reverting task 2 alone re-introduces the browser Sentry SDK and would immediately fail task 6's budgets, so the pair must be reverted together; and reverting task 6 alone restores `continue-on-error: true` on the Lighthouse job, which must be recorded in `docs/architecture.md` §4 again if it happens, or the deferred row is silently lost.
- **Exit signal.** `/status` shows `004 implemented`, Phase 0 progress `4/12` specs; the four locale homes on the preview are branded, headed and footed with a functional consent banner; the `lighthouse` job is green and **required** on five URLs; the bundle table shows every public route under 120 KB gz; axe is 8/8 with no exception list; `docs/architecture.md` §4 has **three** rows left — `ALLOW_PLACEHOLDER_ENV` (spec 002), "no browser Sentry SDK on public routes" (spec 013) and the `deploymentEnvironment()` Railway caveat (spec 007). *Corrected at TASK-056 (§14 A17): this sentence said "one row left", which was never true of spec 004's scope — spec 004 removes the **CSP** row and the `vercel.live` note, which is what AC-30 asserts, and the other two rows belong to specs this one cannot discharge.*

## 13. Open questions

**Resolved 2026-09-08 (founder reviewed the design canvas over five iterations; decisions below are binding):** Q1/A9 brand: the system in `docs/design/homepage-v1/` — white paper (oklch 99.3%), cool-grey inks, one forest-green accent (oklch 42% 0.10 155), the origin-stem-bloom mark (`content/brand/mark.svg`) with a Newsreader wordmark. Q2 fonts: Newsreader (display, 500, roman) + IBM Plex Sans (body), self-hosted Latin + Latin-Ext. Q3 dark mode: token-ready only. Q4/A6 guarantee copy default accepted ("7-day freshness guarantee · redeliver or refund"). Q5 CSP split accepted as ADR-0016 (to be written at task 2). Q6 consent: build. Q7 GA4 gated on env var. Q8 browser Sentry off public routes (already done in spec 003 TASK-043). Q9 `src/modules/ui/`. Q10 env-gated `/dev/components`. Q11/A2 corridor page. Q12/A7 DE, FR, ES, IT, RO, NL. Q13 budget: option (a) — restate as ≤ 120 KB Brotli transfer, and remove zod from the client via a zod-free locale data module for the 500 document. Layout decisions from the canvas that override §5 defaults where they differ: commerce header (logo left, search field + button, labelled Sign in / My orders / Basket, full category row); hero = full-bleed photograph with the finder card (type-ahead country → town/postcode → date → neutral "Continue"); four-fact proof row; occasion tiles; "Most sent this week" row gated on real orders (florists' picks until then, labelled); verified-reviews section hidden until real reviews exist, with a Trustpilot slot; destinations grid with live/guide status; colophon footer with company registration, Stripe-processed payments and market payment methods. Original questions kept below.

- **Q1 (plan/13 A9) — brand assets: is there an existing logo, palette or typeface, or does 004 create a minimal token system?** Default (accepted here, and `plan/13`'s own recommendation): 004 creates it — a warm neutral-grey ramp, one deep botanical green as brand, one warm blush/terracotta accent, a typographic wordmark, and one variable font, all in one file and all subject to the contrast test of AC-3. The cost of answering late is bounded by design: a palette swap is one `@theme` block plus re-running the contrast test and the visual baselines (roughly an hour), and the wordmark is one SVG. What is *not* cheap to change later is the token **naming and structure** (semantic pairs, spacing base, type scale steps), so if the founder has a designer or a brand in mind, this is the week to say so. If the answer is "I will supply assets", 004 ships as specified and a later one-task PR replaces the values.
- **Q2 — which variable font, and is one enough?** Default (specified here): one self-hosted variable font, Inter Variable (SIL OFL, complete Latin-Ext for Polish and the Phase-4 `ro`/`tr` diacritics, good fallback metrics), no display face. `plan/01` §7 permits exactly one. Alternatives worth a sentence: a serif or humanist face would look less like every SaaS site and more like a gift brand, at the cost of finding one with real Latin-Ext coverage and clean variable metrics; a second display face for headings costs ~25–40 KB and a second preload against a 2.0 s LCP budget. Confirm Inter, or name a face — the choice is a file swap in task 1 and a nuisance afterwards.
- **Q3 — dark mode: ship it in Phase 0, or token-ready only?** Default (specified here): **token-ready only**. Semantic token names plus `color-scheme: light` mean a later `prefers-color-scheme` block is one CSS block and zero component edits. Shipping it now doubles the contrast matrix (AC-3), doubles every visual baseline (AC-27) and doubles the axe surface, for a demo whose audience is three Warsaw florists and Googlebot. Cost of deferral: the day a founder wants it, one PR. Confirm, or say "ship both from the start".
- **Q4 (plan/13 A6) — the guarantee's name and exact terms.** Default (accepted here as *copy*): "7-day freshness guarantee · redeliver or refund". It appears in the trust strip and the footer, and it is a single message key (`trust.guarantee.name`) plus a body key, so a rename is a catalogue edit in every locale and not a code change — which is why this does not block implementation. What it *does* block is `plan/07` §2's guarantee page and the terms it references (7 days from delivery? refund in full or partial? redelivery at whose choice? who pays the florist?), so an answer is needed before 007 publishes `/guarantee` and before the first partner agreement. Note for the reviewers of `plan/13` B12: the guarantee name is a brand term whose German and Polish forms must be decided once, not per string.
- **Q5 — CSP: accept the allowlist-on-cached-HTML / nonce-on-no-store split, as ADR-0016?** `plan/01` §9 says "CSP with nonces". A nonce cannot exist in an ISR/SSG response without either making it a public constant (worthless) or rendering every indexable page per request (a direct hit on priority #1 and #2). Default (specified in §5.2): a static per-environment allowlist plus a build-time **hash** for our single ≤1 KB inline consent bootstrap on cached routes, `Report-Only` first with `/api/csp-report` collecting evidence, and a per-request **nonce with `'strict-dynamic'`** on the `no-store` routes (checkout, account, admin, vendor) written by the spec that ships the first one. The trade-off accepted: an XSS that can inject `<script src>` to an allowlisted origin — in practice `googletagmanager.com`, which serves arbitrary container code — is not blocked on marketing pages. Alternatives: (a) nonce everywhere → dynamic rendering, budgets fail, rejected; (b) full hash-based policy → Next's inline bootstrap content varies per build and route, so hashes must be computed per response, which is nonce-generation with extra steps; (c) no CSP until 013 → leaves spec 001's deferred row open through the demo. Confirm the split (and whether the enforce flip happens at the end of Phase 0 or with 013), and whether it is recorded as **ADR-0016**.
- **Q6 — consent: build or buy?** Default (specified here, and `plan/07` §5's recommendation): **build**. Two days of work, zero third-party JS before consent, no per-page pricing (Cookiebot's free tier is page-limited and our URL count passes it immediately), a first-party consent record we own, and full control of the "equal weight" requirement that AC-20 tests. What we give up: a vendor's maintained per-jurisdiction copy and their audit trail, and the Phase-0 record is a log line rather than a table until spec 002 unparks. Confirm, or name a CMP — a bought CMP is a new processor, a RoPA row, a DPA, a third-party script and roughly 30–60 KB of budget.
- **Q7 — when does GA4 actually turn on?** Default (specified here): the loader ships in 004, gated on `NEXT_PUBLIC_GA4_MEASUREMENT_ID`, so it stays dark in CI and preview and lights up the moment the founder pastes the id from the week-1 GA4 property into the production env store — satisfying `plan/09` Phase 0 AC 7 with no code change, while the event schema, the `consent_state` dimension and the server-side `purchase` event stay spec 023's. Two consequences to accept: setting that env var is a **RoPA-affecting act** (Google becomes an active processor and the privacy policy must already name it), and the tag costs ~30–35 KB gz of the 120 KB script budget in production, which is measured in preview only when the id is set. Confirm — or say "GA4 waits for spec 023", in which case Phase 0 AC 7 is met by the consent signals alone and the roadmap AC needs a note.
- **Q8 — accept no browser-side Sentry on public marketing/shop pages in Phase 0?** The client SDK plus the zod it drags in is ~230 KB gz of a 120 KB budget and is why the Lighthouse job has never been green. Default (specified here): remove it from public routes, keep server and edge Sentry untouched, and bring the client SDK back on the checkout routes in spec 013 where a client-side error costs money. Cost: a JavaScript error on a marketing page is invisible until someone reports it. Alternative: keep it and lazy-load it after LCP behind consent — more machinery, a consent question about an error tracker (arguably essential, arguably not), and still budget spend. Confirm.
